{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z^{\prime}}\mathord{R^{\leftrightarrow}_{\mathfrak{G}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
. The latter means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z^{\prime}}\in L_{3}
. By (
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
)-structural saturation,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z^{\prime}}}
. Hence,
L_{1}\leq L_{3}
by Definition
6.2
.

For antisymmetry, let
L_{1}\leq L_{2}
and
L_{1}\neq L_{2}
.
By Definition
6.2
, there are labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in L_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\in L_{2}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
. For arbitrary labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in L_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\in L_{2}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{R^{\leftrightarrow}_{\mathfrak{G}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{R^{\leftrightarrow}_{\mathfrak{G}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}
. Since
L_{1}\neq L_{2}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
by
2
) of Definition
6.2
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\in L_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in L_{1}
, it is not the case that
L_{2}\leq L_{1}
.
∎

\OrderClusters

*

Proof.

For reflexivity of
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
, consider any cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
. For any label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
, by (
R\mathsf{rf}
)-structural saturation,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
by Definition
6.6
.
For transitivity of
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
for clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{2}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{3}
. By Definition
6.6
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
for some labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
, by
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
by Definition
6.6
.
For antisymmetry of
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
.
By Definition
6.6
, there are labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
. For arbitrary labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
, hence, by
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
. Similarly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
. Since both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, all labels in these two clusters form one equivalence class w.r.t.
R_{\mathfrak{G}}\cap R_{\mathfrak{G}}^{-1}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
.

For reflexivity of
\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}
, consider any cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
. For every label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
, by (
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
)-structural saturation,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}}
by Definition
6.6
.
For transitivity of
\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}}
for clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{2}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}_{3}
. By Definition
6.6
, for every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
, there is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
. In its turn, for this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, there is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
. By
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
for this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}}
by Definition
6.6
. Hence,
\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}
is a preorder.

Assume now additionally that
\mathfrak{G}
is layered.
For antisymmetry of
\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}=\varnothing
. To show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}}
, we consider any label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
.
By Definition
6.6
, for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, there is some label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
. We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{R^{\leftrightarrow}_{\mathfrak{G}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
because they belong to the same cluster,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, and hence
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{R^{\leftrightarrow}_{\mathfrak{G}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, by
(R\mathsf{rf})
-structural saturation, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
because they are from disjoint clusters. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
by Definition
6.2
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
was chosen arbitrarily, it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{\not\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}}
and
\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}
is an order. ∎

-D

Proofs from Section
7

For simplicity, when
w\leq v
, then we say that
w
is
a past of
v
and that
v
is
a future of
w
.

\SaturationStable

*

Proof.

To prove
1
assume that set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is finite and all sequents
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
are stable, i.e., tree-layered and tree-clustered (in particular, layered and structurally saturated) with happy inner layers. The size of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}
is larger than that of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
by at most one sequent, hence,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}
is also finite. Since all labels in the inner layers of sequents from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
are happy, labels of all newly added labelled formulas, be it label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
itself or labels
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
-accessible from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, must be in a topmost layer of some sequent from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
. Given that this sequent is layered, the labels of all new labelled formulas have no non-reflexive futures, making (
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
) trivial for them.
Since rewrites neither introduce new labels nor add relational atoms, all other conditions of structural saturation, as well as being being tree-layered and tree-clustered, remain true.
Since no labelled formulas have been removed and all inner layers of all sequents remain unchanged, the inner layers remain happy (see Remark
6.10
). Therefore, all sequents in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}
are stable, and so is
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}
itself.

To prove
2
, observe that each rewrite adds a labelled formula to a sequent
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
if and only if the formula is not already in
\mathfrak{G}
.
Moreover, the added formulas are subformulas
of formulas in the sequent, and no new labels are introduced.
Since each
\mathfrak{G}
contains finitely many formulas and finitely many labels, only finitely many formulas can be added to the sequent. Thus, if there are finitely many sequents in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, relation
\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}
is terminating.

Finally, to prove
3
, suppose first that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is semi-saturated. Then, by definition,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is finite, all sequents in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
are stable, in particular, have happy inner layers, and have naively happy topmost layers. Since all these finitely many sequents are stable,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is stable. Since all their layers are (at least) naively happy, no rewrite step is applicable to
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, so
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is in normal form w.r.t.
\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}
. Conversely, suppose that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is stable and in normal form w.r.t.
\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}
. Then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is a finite set of stable sequents. Since no rewrite is applicable to any sequent from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, all (topmost) layers of all these sequents are naively happy. Hence, all finitely many sequents from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
are semi-saturated, and so is
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
itself.∎

The following two lemmas are not in the main text and are needed to prove Lemma
7.6
.

Lemma .5
.

The following statements hold:

1.

If a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
is semi-saturated and
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, then the sequent
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is stable.

2.

If a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
is semi-saturated and
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
according to option
1
, then the sequent
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is semi-saturated.

Proof.

To prove
1
assume that a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
is semi-saturated, in particular, stable (i.e., structurally saturated, tree-layered, and tree-clustered with happy inner layers). Let
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. For either option from Def.
7.5
, the unhappy label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
can only be in a topmost layer of
\mathfrak{G}
. Both labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
from option
1
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
from option
2
belong to the same topmost layer. Hence, in either case neither labels nor most labelled formulas from the inner layers are affected and, therefore, they remain happy by Remark
6.10
. For option
2
, no formulas or labels are removed so all white
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
formulas from the inner layers remain happy. For option
1
, formulas
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
from inner layers remain happy because
y
has no past. Finally, it is possible that
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
from some inner layer is happy because of
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
. But since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, also
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
. Note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
has no past. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
means that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
remains happy. For option
1
, there are no new labels and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}
, hence, the only change in
\leq
-relational atoms is that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
turns into
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
;
for option
2
, the only new label is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
being the only new
\leq
-relational atom. Either way, the
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf})
- and
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation is ensured and the set of irreflexive
\leq
-relational atoms remains unchanged. The latter implies that the
(\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet})
-,
(\mathsf{F_{1}})
-,
and
(\mathsf{F_{2}})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
follows from the same properties of
\mathfrak{G}
. The
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation is explicitly enforced. So is
(R\mathsf{rf})
-structural saturation for option
2
, where it is necessary for the added label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
. This completes the proof that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is structurally saturated. As the rewrite does not affect the layer structure of the sequent,
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
being tree-layered immediately follows from the same property of
\mathfrak{G}
.
To show that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is tree-clustered we consider the two options separately. In option
1
of Def.
7.5
, the rewrite performs a substitution of label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
for label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and applies the transitive closure. By considering the shortest chains justifying new
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-links, and using the structural saturation of both
\mathfrak{G}
and
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
along with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, it follows that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\quad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y};

(5)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\quad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v};

(6)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\text{ but not }{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\quad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}.

(7)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
be the cluster in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
that contains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
. For any cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
in
\mathfrak{G}
, either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
and either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
remains a cluster in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
. The case of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
is easy.
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
, which is not a cluster in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
. Since all
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
-links unrelated to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
are preserved in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
can only grow. So there must be some labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
, but either not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
or not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
. By (
7
), in the former case,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
, hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
, while in the latter case,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
, hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
. Either way, by structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
.
To show that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is tree-clustered (in option
1
of Def.
7.5
), we consider
three clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
from
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
and show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-comparable. If any two of them are equal, this is trivial, so assume they are pairwise distinct. If none of them is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
, then they are also clusters in
\mathfrak{G}
and it is sufficient to show that there is some cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}
in
\mathfrak{G}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
-comparable because
\mathfrak{G}
is tree-clustered and the connection remains in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, meaning that they are also
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-comparable.
If both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
. If neither
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
nor
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
, then
it follows from (
7
) that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
is the cluster in
\mathfrak{G}
that contains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
. So
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
. Finally, if, w.l.o.g.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
but not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
by (
7
) where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
is the cluster in
\mathfrak{G}
that contains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
. Since
\mathfrak{G}
is tree-clustered, either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
. In the latter case,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
. In the former case, we derive the
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-connection
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
directly from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
.
It remains to consider the case when exactly one of the three clusters is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
and the other two are clusters of
\mathfrak{G}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
by (
5
), so the above argument with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
suffices.
Finally, if w.l.o.g.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
by (
6
). If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
is
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
-comparable to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
because
\mathfrak{G}
is tree-clustered, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
is
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-comparable to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
but not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
by (
7
), and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
. The situation in option
2
of Def.
7.5
is much simpler: there a single new cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
is added to the clusters of
\mathfrak{G}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
. The only non-trivial new case to consider is when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
, in which case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{y}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are
R_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}
-comparable because they are
R_{\mathfrak{G}}
-comparable.
This completes the proof that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is tree-clustered and overall stable.

To prove
2
, since most of the conditions were just shown in
1
, it is sufficient to additionally show that all topmost layers of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
are naively happy. Since option
1
adds neither new formulas nor new labels, for most types of formulas their (naive) happiness is inherited from
\mathfrak{G}
. The only exceptions are
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
- and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
-formulas. The argument is the same for both. We show it for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas. Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
and
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}C}^{\bullet}
. Since no formulas are added in option
1
, also
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}C}^{\bullet}
. There are two possibilities: either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
already in
\mathfrak{G}
or it was added to ensure the structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
. In the former case,
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!v}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}^{\bullet}
and
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!v}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}C}^{\bullet}
by the naive happiness of this layer in
\mathfrak{G}
. In the latter case, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
but not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
, hence, by (
7
), both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box C}^{\bullet}
naive happiness of the layer in
\mathfrak{G}
, so
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box C}^{\bullet}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
in
\mathfrak{G}
, and
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!v}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}^{\bullet}
and
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!v}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}C}^{\bullet}
, again due to naive happiness. Both
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}C^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
remain at
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
. ∎

Lemma .6
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}
be a semi-saturated sequent, let
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, and let
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
be a semi-saturation of
\{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\}
. Then all happy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas are preserved by the transition from
\mathfrak{G}
to
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
, i.e., for all sequents
\mathfrak{G^{*}}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
and for all labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
occurring in
\mathfrak{G^{*}}
, if
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
is happy, then so is

\mathfrak{G^{*}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Note that semi-saturation does not remove formulas or labels, nor does it add
R
-relational atoms. Hence, semi-saturation in option
2
cannot spoil happiness of any
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula, and it is sufficient to show that no happy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula of
\mathfrak{G}
becomes unhappy in
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
. For option
2
, this is trivial as, again, nothing is removed. For option
1
, the only problems relate to the removal of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and could potentially occur if
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
was happy because of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}^{\bullet}
. However, in this case,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}^{\bullet}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
in
\mathfrak{G}
, thus, happiness persists.
∎

\DiaSaturationStable

*

Proof.

To prove
1

assume that set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is finite and all sequents in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
are semi-saturated (in particular, stable). Observe that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}=\bigl(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\setminus\{\mathfrak{G}\}\bigr)\cup\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
for some
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
where
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
is as in Lemma
.6
.
In Option
1
, we replace one semi-saturated sequent
\mathfrak{G}
with another one sequent
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, which is semi-saturated by Lemma
.5
.
2
. Hence, the resulting set is semi-saturated. In option
2
, sequent
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is stable by Lemma
.5
.
1
. By Lemma
7.2
, set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\ast}
is semi-saturated. Hence, so is
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
.

To prove
2
, consider a sequence of sets
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{1}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{2}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}\dots
. Let us divide all labels occurring in this sequence into
old
, i.e., occurring in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, and
new
, i.e., introduced later. We also call unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas old or new based on their label. The number of old unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas can never increase, and each
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
transition makes at least one
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula (old or new) happy. Hence, it is sufficient to show that it is impossible to continue creating new labels indefinitely.
Using tree-clusteredness, we define the
new-cluster-depth
of a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
(from a topmost layer of a sequent from one of the sets) to be the length of the shortest chain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}o}R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}R_{\mathfrak{G}}\dots R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}o}
is an old label while labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{0}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}
are new. In particular, the new-cluster-depth of any old label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}o}
is
0
. To prove termination we consider a Derschowitz–Manna-style ordering on multisets of new-cluster-depths of all new unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
. Let us denote such a multiset of integers by
\mathsf{unh}_{\mathord{\Diamond}}(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i})
and pair it with the number of old unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
. Let the
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i+1}
be performed based on an unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
at label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
with new-cluster-depth
k
. If
k=0
, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
is an old label, then the number of old unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas decreases. It is easy to show by induction on
i
that for new
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, i.e., for
k>0
, label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
forms a singleton cluster
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\}
any non-trivial
R
-children of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
must be
R
-leaves in the respective topmost layer. Thus, since by part
.6
all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas outside of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
preserve happiness, all formulas in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
disappear, and other clusters with unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas preserve their new-cluster-depth, multiset
\mathsf{unh}_{\mathord{\Diamond}}(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i+1})\subsetneq\mathsf{unh}_{\mathord{\Diamond}}(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i})
, i.e., at least one unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
at label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, disappeared, no additional unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas appeared, and no unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas decreased their new-cluster-depth. Alternatively, if
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
is obtained via option
2
, then, again by
.6
, no additional unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas appear in labels present in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
and some unhappy ones may become happy. However, the semi-saturation can create new unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas in the newly created label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
, which has cluster-depth
k+1
(it is easy to see that other labels are unaffected by the semi-saturation). Thus,

\mathsf{unh}_{\mathord{\Diamond}}(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{{i+1}})\subseteq\mathsf{unh}_{\mathord{\Diamond}}(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i})\setminus\{k\}\cup\{k+1,\dots,k+1\}
where the multiplicity of added
k+1
depends on how many unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas are in
z
after semi-saturation. To ensure termination, it remains to note that there is a global upper bound on new-cluster-depths of unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas. Indeed, let
M
be the number of formulas that are subformulas of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
. We show that no unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula can have new-cluster-depth exceeding
2^{M}+1
. This follows from the fact that, due to the subformula property, there are at most
2^{M}
distinct sets of formulas assigned to one label. All new labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
created in option
2
have no past, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas in a child of such
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
are not used for saturation until
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
is almost happy. Consider two such new labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}
. Suppose an unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}
was chosen for the next saturation step. Then
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}\}
is a singleton cluster and all its non-trivial parents, including
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}
are almost happy. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}
, then option
1
would dictate to identify
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}
. Thus, for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{2}}
to remain a separate cluster, it has to be different from all the labels in its strictly preceding clusters of new labels. Thus, for any sequence of distinct clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}R_{\mathfrak{G}}\dots R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{k}}
of new labels, there must be labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{1}}R_{\mathfrak{G}}\dots R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{k}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{j}}\not\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z_{l}}
for any
j\neq l
.
It means that after at most
k\geq 2^{M}
clusters of new labels in a row, the (semi-saturated) new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
would be equivalent to some of the previously created labels and option
1
would prevent extending the sequence further. Since all new unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas are pushed in the direction of increasing their new-cluster-depth, which cannot exceed the global bound of
2^{M}+1
and the number of old unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas only decreases,
we conclude that
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
terminates.

To prove
3
, observe that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
being saturated by definition means that it is semi-saturated and all topmost layers of its sequents are almost happy. A layer is almost happy if and only if it is naively happy and all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formulas occurring in it are happy. Saturation
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
can be applied to a semi-saturated set iff it has an unhappy
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
-formula (in its topmost layer). Hence,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is saturated iff it is semi-saturated and in normal form w.r.t.
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
.
∎

\LiftingSaturationStable

*

Proof.

Assume that
\mathfrak{G}
is saturated. Then it is stable, i.e., structurally saturated, tree-layered, and tree-clustered with happy inner layers, and, in addition, has almost happy topmost layers.
By definition,
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
consists of
\mathfrak{G}
to which we add one topmost layer
{\hat{L}}
for each unhappy formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box A}
occurring in some not simulated topmost layer
L
of
\mathfrak{G}
(these formerly topmost layers become inner in
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
).
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{x\}}
is a singleton cluster, such a layer
{\hat{L}}
is a copy of
L
(with an additional label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box A}
); as can be seen in Fig.
5
, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to a non-singleton cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
, layer
{\hat{L}}
additionally contains a copy of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
and a label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
(and an additional label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box A}
).
Since each of these added layers is topmost, all conditions can be verified for each of them independently.
As mentioned earlier, Construction
7.8
(and Construction
7.9
for the additional label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
) explicitly ensures that
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
is layered, that the new layers
{\hat{L}}
of
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
enjoy the
(R\mathsf{rf})
- and
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation, and that
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
satisfies the
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf})
-,
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-,

(\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet})
-,
(\mathsf{F_{1}})
-,
and
(\mathsf{F_{2}})
-structural saturation.
The fact that
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
is tree-layered follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{G}
is tree-layered and, for each added topmost layer
{\hat{L}}
, we have
L^{\prime}<{\hat{L}}
iff
L^{\prime}\leq L
.
Showing that
{\hat{L}}
is tree-clustered in case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to a singleton cluster is immediate, as the
R
-structure of
{\hat{L}}
is either isomorphic to that of the tree-clustered
L
or is obtained from it by adding one singleton cluster
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\}
that is a leaf w.r.t.
R
.
In case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to a non-singleton cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
, it is easy to see from Construction
7.8
and Construction
7.9
that
{\hat{L}}
is partitioned into the following clusters:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}_{j}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}_{x}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}
, which we call
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
-clusters, as well as clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y_{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\}
for each cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
of
L
, which we call
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-clusters and, in case
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box A}
, additionally cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
(see Fig.
5
). It is equally easy to see for arbitrary
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}^{*}}
that:

1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}^{*}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{*}}
,

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}_{x}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
(also iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
in presence of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
),

3.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}_{x}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
(but never
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
),

4.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}_{x}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}_{x}}}
are the only non-reflexive relations among
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
-clusters,

5.

in presence of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}^{\prime}_{x}}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
are the only remaining relations involving
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
.

Consider any three of these clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
from
{\hat{L}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
. We need to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are comparable in
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
. Omitting some trivial cases, assume that the three clusters are pairwise distinct.
We distinguish cases depending on whether these clusters are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
-clusters,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-clusters, etc.
The case of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
reduces to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{i}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{i}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\hat{x}\}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
, the latter case being trivial. All other cases involving
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
are also trivial. The case when both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
-clusters is trivial because they are all pairwise comparable in
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
. Thus, it is sufficient to consider the cases when none of the clusters is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
and, w.l.o.g.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-cluster.
All non-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{z\}}
clusters in
{\hat{L}}
have origin clusters in
L
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
for a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{C}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
for each of the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
clusters. Let us call the origins of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
clusters
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{2}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{3}}
respectively. Considering all four possibilities on whether
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{3}}
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
-clusters or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
-clusters, one can see that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{3}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{2}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{3}}
. Since
\mathfrak{G}
is tree-clustered,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{o}_{2}}
are comparable in
\mathfrak{G}
. And this implies that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
are comparable in
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
in each of the four cases.
This concludes the proof that
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
is tree-clustered.

Finally, since
\mathfrak{G}
is saturated, all its topmost layers are almost happy, that is, all formulas that are not of the shape
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
are happy. By adding new topmost layers without removing any formulas or any labels we make all of them happy, without making any other formula of
\mathfrak{G}
unhappy (here we rely on the local formulation of happiness for
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}^{\bullet}
in Def.
5.3
). Thus, all inner layers of
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
are happy, and
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
is stable.
∎

\LoopSaturationStable

*

Proof.

To prove
1
assume that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is saturated. Let us consider a sequent
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
such that
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\circlearrowright}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. Then,
\mathfrak{G}
contains either an unhappy R-triangle loop or an unhappy U-triangle loop. Sequent
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\mathfrak{G}
by substituting label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
for all occurrences of label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
and then closing
R
under transitivity (Definition
7.18
).
By assumption,
\mathfrak{G}
is stable, i.e., it is structurally saturated, tree-layered, and tree clustered with happy inner layers.
Irrespective of which kind of triangle loop occurs in
\mathfrak{G}
, labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
belong to the same topmost layer of the sequent. Hence, neither labels nor labelled formulas from inner layers are affected by the rewrite and, by Remark
6.10
, their happiness is preserved for most formulas. There are two types of formulas that might be exceptions. We consider them separately. For the first type, if
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}^{\circ}
from some inner layer is happy because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
,
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!t}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
, and
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!t}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}^{\circ}
, then both
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!s}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
and
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!s}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}^{\circ}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
. Thus,
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}^{\circ}
remains happy because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
. For the other type, consider the situation where
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
from some inner layer is happy because
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!v}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
where one or both of labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
. If only
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, then
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!s}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}

make
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
happy. If only
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
make
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
happy. Finally, if both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, then
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!s}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
make
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!k}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\circ}
happy. In all cases, all formulas in inner layers remain happy.
The rewrite does not introduce new labels, and the only changes in
\leq
-relational atoms are that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
turns into
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}}
and, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
. The
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
is explicitly enforced, while the
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf})
- and
(R\mathsf{rf})
-structural saturation are not affected by the removal of label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
. The only non-trivial case for
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation is when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
belong to unaffected layers, hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}}
. Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
by
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
by construction. The
(\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet})
-structural saturation follows from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
.
Finally, for
(\mathsf{F_{1}})
-,
and
(\mathsf{F_{2}})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
, the most non-trivial case is when, for one of the two,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
. Again
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
belong to unaffected layers, hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}k}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}
. By the same structural saturation property of
\mathfrak{G}
, there exists a label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
, so the same label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
fulfills the conditions. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
, which together with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
fulfills the conditions. The remaining case is symmetric.
This concludes the proof that
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is structurally saturated.
Since the rewrite does not affect the layer structure of the sequent, we immediately conclude that
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is tree-layered.
The proof that
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is tree-clustered proceeds in the same way as the proof of case
1
of Lemma
7.6
, in the case of option
1
of Definition
7.5
. This concludes the proof that
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is stable.
By assumption,
\mathfrak{G}
is saturated. This means that all topmost layers of
\mathfrak{G}
are almost happy, that is, all formulas that are not of the shape
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
are happy.
It remains to show that all topmost layers of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
are almost happy. Almost happiness of labelled propositional formulas of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is local and, hence, is not affected by the new
R
- and
\leq
-links. Happiness of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
- and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
-formulas is shown as in the proof of case
1
of Lemma
7.6
.

To prove
2
, observe that, unlike in the case of
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
(Definition
7.5
),
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\circlearrowright}}
never introduces new labels in the sequents on which it operates. Whenever
\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\circlearrowright}}
is applied to a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
, the number of labels occurring in one of the topmost layers of
\mathfrak{G}
is reduced by one because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
.
Therefore, for any topmost layer
L
in
\mathfrak{G}
containing
n\geq 1
labels, transformation
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\circlearrowright}}
can be applied at most
n-1
times to the labels occurring in
L
. Since saturated set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is finite, the rewrite
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\circlearrowright}}
is terminating.
∎

Proofs from Section

\countermodel

*

Proof.

If the algorithm terminates in Step 4, then
\mathfrak{G}_{i}\mathord{\uparrow}=\mathfrak{G}_{i}
for some non-axiomatic sequent
\mathfrak{G}_{i}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
. It is easy to see that label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
, the only label in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{0}
, is never removed by the algorithm because it always remains in the root cluster of the root layer of every sequent in every set. Indeed: semi-saturation does not remove labels; saturation does not remove labels from root clusters; lifting saturation does not remove labels; finally, looping saturation does not remove labels from the root layer. Hence,
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!r}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}^{\circ}
. For each unhappy topmost layer
L
of
\mathfrak{G_{i}}
, there is some inner layer
L^{\prime}
simulating
L
(see Definition
7.11
) via a simulation
\mathsf{S}_{L}
. Let
\mathfrak{G}
be obtained by first adding to
\mathfrak{G_{i}}
all relational atoms
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathsf{S}_{L}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
for some unhappy topmost layer
L
and then closing the result under transitivity of
\leq
. It is sufficient to show that
\mathfrak{G}
is happy. Then by Theorem
5.8
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}\not\Vdash{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
. Therefore, by Theorem
2.5
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
is not a theorem in
\mathsf{IS4}
.

We need to show that
\mathfrak{G}
is structurally saturated and all its formulas are happy. Since no new labels were added,
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf})
- and
(R\mathsf{rf})
-structural saturation is preserved.
(R\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation is preserved because no new
R
-links were added.
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation is explicitly enforced. To show that
(\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet})
-,
(\mathsf{F_{1}})
-, and
(\mathsf{F_{2}})
-structural saturation are preserved, it is sufficient to demonstrate them for each of the
\leq
-links added before the transitive closure. For the
(\mathsf{F_{1}})
-,
and
(\mathsf{F_{2}})
-structural saturation these are exactly the simulation conditions
\mathsf{S1}
and
\mathsf{S2}
from Definition
7.11
. For the
(\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet})
-simulation this follows from the fact that
\mathsf{S}_{L}\subseteq\sim
for all
L
, which means that whenever
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathsf{S}_{L}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
for some
L
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
and, hence,
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x^{\prime}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
. This completes the proof that
\mathfrak{G}
is structurally saturated. Since no formulas or links were removed, all formulas happy in
\mathfrak{G_{i}}
remain happy in
\mathfrak{G}
(here we again rely on the local formulation of happiness for
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}^{\bullet}
in Def.
5.3
). All formulas from the inner layers of saturated sequent
\mathfrak{G_{i}}
were happy, as were all formulas apart from possibly some
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
of one of the two shapes
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
in topmost layers. For any such unhappy
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}^{\circ}
from some topmost layer
L
of
\mathfrak{G_{i}}
, there is an inner layer
L^{\prime}
and, by Prop.
7.12
, there is a label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\in L^{\prime}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathsf{S}_{L}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
. At the same time
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
so
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x^{\prime}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}^{\circ}
is happy in its inner layer. If
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
, we must have
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!z}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
,
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!z}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}^{\circ}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G_{i}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}}
by
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G}
, which makes
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}^{\circ}
happy. Finally, if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\Box A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
, we must have
\mathfrak{G_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!z}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\circ}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G_{i}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G_{i}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}
by
(\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr})
-structural saturation of
\mathfrak{G}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
by construction, which makes
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}^{\circ}
happy.
This completes the proof that
\mathfrak{G}
is a happy sequent.
∎

Proofs from Section
