\daggeradm

*

Proof.

Whenever the rule of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}
that corresponds to
\mathsf{r}
removes the principal formula, we use first the contraction rule to create a second copy. Then, rules of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
adding formulas to the antecedent are emulated by the respective rule of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}
followed by
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
to propagate the antecedent formula upward.

The reflexive relational atoms
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\leq}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
in
\mathbin{\supset}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
and
\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet}
are present in the conclusion by the assumption that the conclusion is a proper sequent.

The composite rule
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
is emulated by using
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}
to create label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
, followed by a sequence of
\mathsf{F_{2}}
rules to create other labels from
\hat{U}
(it is important to perform these rules in the order compatible with the order on layers, i.e., without skipping intermediate layers), followed by
R\mathsf{rf}
and
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
for all these created labels, followed by instances of
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
to add all requisite
\leq
-relational atoms (relying on the
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
-structural saturation of the conclusion sequent), followed by instances of
R\mathsf{tr}
to add all requisite
R
-relational atoms, followed by instances of admissible
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
to propagate
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}
upward.

The composite rule
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
is emulated by using
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}
to create label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
, followed by a sequence of
\mathsf{F_{1}}
and
\mathsf{F_{2}}
rules to create other labels from
{\hat{L}}
(it is important to perform these rules in the order of increasing distance from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
in layer
L
, i.e., without skipping intermediate labels), followed by
R\mathsf{rf}
and
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
for all these created labels, followed by instances of
R\mathsf{tr}
to add all requisite
R
-relational atoms (relying on the
R\mathsf{tr}
-structural saturation in layer
L
), followed by instances of
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
to add all requisite
\leq
-relational atoms, followed by instances of admissible
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
to lift all succedent formulas of
L
into
{\hat{L}}
.

The composite rule
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
is emulated in the same way, only using
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}
to create
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
in place of
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}
. Note that here instances of
R\mathsf{tr}
would additionally add
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
was present in the conclusion sequent because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is created as in the case of
\mathbin{\supset}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ}
, while
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
is created by rule
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}
.
∎

\properrestrict

*

Proof.

We also prove that for every instance of a unary or binary rule
\mathsf{r}\notin\{\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star},\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}\}
in
\mathcal{D}
, the label of the principal formula is in a topmost layer (of both the conclusion and the premise(s)).

The proposition and this statement are
proved by mutual induction on the given tidy derivation. It is sufficient to consider each rule
\mathsf{r}
of
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
separately. Let
\mathfrak{G}
be the conclusion of an instance of
\mathsf{r}
in
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathfrak{H}
be (one of) its premise(s).

\mathsf{r}\in\{\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet},\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ},\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet},\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ},\mathbin{\supset}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet},\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet},\mathord{\Diamond}^{\circ},\mathsf{4}^{\bullet},\mathsf{4}^{\circ}\}

These rules create neither new labels nor new relational atoms, and have
\mathcal{f}(\mathfrak{H})=\mathcal{f}(\mathfrak{G})
by (
4
). Hence, most aspects of properness for
\mathfrak{H}
are trivially inherited from
\mathfrak{G}
, including verticality, lack of non-singleton clusters, tree-clusteredness and tree-layeredness, as well as most aspects of structural completeness, with the exception of
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
, which potentially could have been violated by adding formulas to inner layers. However, the tidiness of
\mathcal{D}
means that all added formulas have been added to labels that are allowed, which in restricted sequents can only be in topmost layers. In other words, whenever rule
\mathsf{r}
adds a labelled formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
, label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
has no non-trivial futures, i.e., there are no labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
. Thus, sequent
\mathfrak{H}
is
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
-saturated as well, and, hence, proper. Finally, since the label of the principal formula for all these rules is in the same layer as the label(s) to which formulas are added (the same label for
\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
,
\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ}
,
\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
,
\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ}
, and
\mathbin{\supset}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
or a parent label for
\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet}
,
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\circ}
,
\mathsf{4}^{\bullet}
, and
\mathsf{4}^{\circ}
), the principal formula is also in a topmost layer.

\mathsf{r}=\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
be the principal formula of the rule. Although this rule creates a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
forming a singleton cluster, it is added to the existing layer
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
and has no past in
\mathfrak{H}
(nor non-trivial futures). Thus, tree-layeredness, verticality, and singleton-cluster conditions are all preserved. Note that by (
4
) the new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
must be allowed in
\mathfrak{H}
, which by definition of restrictedness implies that
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
is topmost (in both
\mathfrak{G}
and
\mathfrak{H}
). The relational atoms added by the rule clearly ensure tree-clusteredness,
R\mathsf{tr}
-,
R\mathsf{rf}
-, and
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
-structural completeness for the newly created label, whereas
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
-structural completeness is trivial.
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
-structural completeness is satisfied since no formulas have been added to labels existing in
\mathfrak{G}
, whereas the only new
\leq
-relational atom is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
for which
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
-structural completeness is trivial.
\mathsf{F_{1}}
- and
\mathsf{F_{2}}
-structural completeness for this new atom
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
are similarly trivial. As for the new
R
-atoms, they all are of the form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
where both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
are in the topmost layer
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
of
\mathfrak{H}
. Since neither has non-trivial futures,
\mathsf{F_{1}}
- and
\mathsf{F_{2}}
-structural completeness is fulfilled.

\mathsf{r}\in\{\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star},\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}\}

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
be the principal formula of the rule and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
be one of the new labels created by the rule such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
. All the labels created by this rule are in the same layer
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
so that
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{H}}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
and this layer becomes a new topmost layer. All newly created labels (save, in the case of
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
, for one label) have past. Clearly, tree-layeredness, verticality,
\mathsf{F_{1}}
-,
\mathsf{F_{2}}
-,
\mathsf{mon}^{\bullet}
-,
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
- and
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{tr}
-structural completeness for the newly created labels are all ensured. The tree-clusteredness for the new layer, as well as,
R\mathsf{tr}
- and
R\mathsf{rf}
-structural completeness for the new layer
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
is inherited from those for
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
(modulo an additional label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
created by
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
in a way that ensures all these properties). Note that, since the rule only touches newly created labels, which are created in a new, topmost layer, it is not necessary that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
is in a topmost layer. ∎

In order to proof Lemma
.9
, we need another definition and an auxiliary lemma.

Definition .7
.

Let a derivation
\mathcal{D}_{n}
be an
n
-unfolding of a stable set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, with premises
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{k}
being
n
-unfoldings of a specific sequent
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
. Let
\mathsf{U}_{i}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{G})\times\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{i}})
be
n
-unfolding relations witnessing that
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{i}
is an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
for each
i=1..k
.
We say that
\mathcal{D}_{n}
is
allowed
for a label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\ell(\mathfrak{G})
iff for every
i=1..k
, every label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\in\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{i}})
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is allowed in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{i}
.

Lemma .8
.

Assume that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
, that
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is the sequent replaced in the rewriting step, and that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}
is the unhappy formula that is expanded according to Def.
7.1
. If
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable
such that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
there is an
N
-unfolding
\mathcal{D}_{N}
of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
that is allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
is also
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable
.

Proof.

We need to show that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable, i.e., for every
n
there is a tidy derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
such that each premise of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
is an
n
-unfolding of some sequent from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
.

It is sufficient to consider each way that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
can be obtained from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
by steps listed in Def.
7.1
.
For each case of Def.
7.1
, we pick some
N\geq n
and extend
\mathcal{D}_{N}
to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
.
The only sequent from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
that is not present in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
is
\mathfrak{G}
, which is replaced in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
either with sequent
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
or with two sequents
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
, depending on the step. Hence, the only premises of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
that have to be extended are the
N
-unfoldings of
\mathfrak{G}
. All the other premises are
N
-unfoldings and, hence,
n
-unfoldings of some (stable) sequent from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
that is still present in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{N}
is allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, for each premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
that is an
N
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
, there is an
N
-unfolding relation
\mathsf{U}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{G})\times\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\notin\mathcal{f}(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})
whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
.
We will break up the steps of Def.
7.1
into substeps that only add one or two formulas to one particular label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
.

The only unfolding condition from Def.
9.3
that prevents
\mathsf{U}
from making
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
an
N
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
(and of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
) is
1
, and only with regard to labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
is the only label of
\mathfrak{G}
with formulas added. Additionally, when
\mathsf{U}
is modified,
5
–
6
need to checked. Every time we extend the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{N}
, the set of forbidden labels for the premise of each added rule must be the same as for the rule’s conclusion to preserve tidiness.

1.

Suppose
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\and B}^{\bullet}
and the missing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
were added to obtain
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. We use
N=n
, set
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}
, which trivially ensures
5
–
6
.
Using the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
implies that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is allowed and that
\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\and B}^{\bullet}
by
1
for
\mathfrak{G}
, to obtain
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}
we extend premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
by rules

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\and B},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\scriptstyle\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\and B}\Longrightarrow\Delta

for each label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
.
It is easy to see that
\mathsf{U}
makes
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}
and
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
because

whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
.

2.

The case when
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}^{\circ}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{\circ}}

used instead of
\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
.

3.

Suppose
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\bullet}
. We further break this step into substeps consisting of adding the missing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
.
Note that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}

for each label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})
due to

being proper.
The sequence of rules
depends on the position of

relative to

:

(a)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, use
N=n
and set
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}
. Again, the only problem to be fixed is
1
. Each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
such that

for
\mathfrak{G}

\scriptstyle\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
with

. Thus,
{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
.

(b)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
are not in the same cluster, again use
N=n
and set
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}
. By
5
–
6
, there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}

. Since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is allowed in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
. By
2
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. By Def.
9.6
1
, all these
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
are also allowed in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
. By
1
for
\mathfrak{G}
, we have
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\bullet}
. Again

is the only problem to be fixed. To obtain

, we extend premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
by rules

\mathcal{R},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\scriptstyle\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet},\mathsf{4}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

for each label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. This ensures that
{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
.

(c)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
are in the same cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
, it is not a singleton cluster. Let
\left|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\right|=k\geq 2
. Use
N=n\mathord{+}1
.
By
6
for
\mathfrak{G}
, sequent

contains

{{\hat{C}}_{1}},\dots,{{\hat{C}}_{n\mathord{+}1}}

is a proper sequent).
In particular, there is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\in{{\hat{C}}_{1}}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}})
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}
and there are

for

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}
for

.
Since
\mathcal{D}_{n+1}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}

\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}

By

for
\mathfrak{G}

\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}^{\bullet}
. Since the proper sequent

must be

-saturated, it follows from

for
\mathfrak{G}
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}
for

9.6

, all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}

are also allowed. Here, we restore
1
only for
n
labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}
unfolding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
. To obtain
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}
, we extend premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of

by rules

\mathcal{R},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\scriptstyle\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet},\mathsf{4}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Box}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

for each

. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{1}}

\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}\setminus\Bigl\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}}\notin\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}\}\Bigr\}.

For each singleton cluster of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
,
5
for this
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}
follows from
5
for the same cluster of
\mathfrak{G}
for
\mathsf{U}
. All non-singleton clusters of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
other than

have

unfolded copies by

for the
(n\mathord{+}1)
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
, and any

of them satisfy

requisite for an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. Finally, for cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
, label sets
