serve as the requisite
n
unfolded copies, in particular,
{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
for
j=2..n\mathord{+}1
.

4.

The case when
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{\circ}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}{A}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}{\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
were added for some label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
is analogous, with
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\circ}
and
\mathsf{4}^{\circ}
used instead of
\mathord{\Box}^{\bullet}
and
\mathsf{4}^{\bullet}
respectively.

5.

Suppose
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}^{\bullet}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
. The sequence of rules depends on the size of the cluster that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to in
\mathfrak{G}
:

(a)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{x\}}
is a singleton cluster, use
N=n
and set
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}
. By
5
for
\mathfrak{G}
, there is a unique
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is allowed in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
. By
1
for
\mathfrak{G}
, we have
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}^{\bullet}
for this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. Again
1
is the only problem to be fixed, which for this rule can be done in two ways: by either adding
A^{\bullet}
towards an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
or adding
B^{\bullet}
towards an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
. We extend premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
by one rule

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\scriptstyle\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}\Longrightarrow\Delta

for the unique label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. The resulting left premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}
and right premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime\prime}
can easily be seen to be
n
-unfoldings of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
respectively, in particular,

{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}^{\prime\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}.

(b)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
for some cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
with
\left|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\right|=k\geq 2
, use
N=2n\mathord{-}1
. By
6
for
\mathfrak{G}
, sequent
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
contains
2n\mathord{-}1
unfolded copies
{{\hat{C}}_{1}},\dots,{{\hat{C}}_{2n\mathord{-}1}}
of all labels from cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
. In particular, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{j}}\in{\hat{C}}_{j}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})
for
j=1..2n\mathord{-}1
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{j}}
for
j=1..2n\mathord{-}1
.
Since
\mathcal{D}_{2n\mathord{-}1}
is allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, all labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{j}}
for
j=1..2n\mathord{-}1
are allowed in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
.
By
1
for
\mathfrak{G}
, we have
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}^{\bullet}
for
j=1..2n\mathord{-}1
. We will not be able to fix
1
for all
2n\mathord{-}1
labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{j}}
, but only for (at least)
n
of them.
We set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}\}
to be the set of
2^{0}=1
premises of the current derivation. Note that
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
is the special case (with
l=0
) of sequents of the form

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{l}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{l}}\Longrightarrow\Delta

(8)

where
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{i}}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}\}
for each
i=1..l
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\vee}B}\in\Gamma
for all
j=1..2n\mathord{-}1
. Starting with
k=0
and repeating the process until
k=2n\mathord{-}2
, we extend each of the
2^{k}
derivation premises of form (
8
) for
l=k
in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{k}
as follows

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k+1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k+1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}\Longrightarrow\Delta

\scriptstyle\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}\;

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{k}}\Longrightarrow\Delta

thus, obtaining
2^{k\mathord{+}1}
derivation premises of form (
8
) for
l=k\mathord{+}1
that form the set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{k\mathord{+}1}
. In the end, one premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
spawns the set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{2n\mathord{-}1}
of
2^{2n\mathord{-}1}
premises of form (
8
) for
l=2n\mathord{-}1
. It remains to show how, for each of the premises from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{2n\mathord{-}1}
, to restrict relation
\mathsf{U}
in a way that produces an
n
-unfolding of either
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
or
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
. Consider any
\mathfrak{H}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{2n\mathord{-}1}
. It has the form

\mathcal{R},\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{2n\mathord{-}1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{2n\mathord{-}1}}\Longrightarrow\Delta

Since all
2n\mathord{-}1
formulas
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{j}}
are repetitions of only two distinct formulas
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}
, by the pigeonhole principle, one of these two formulas must be repeated at least
n
times. In other words
there is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}E}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}\}
such that there are
n
indices
1\leq i_{1}<\dots<i_{n}\leq 2n\mathord{-}1
with
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}D_{i_{j}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}E}
for all
j=1..n
. In this case, for this premise
\mathfrak{H}
, we set

\mathsf{U}_{\mathfrak{H}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}\setminus\Bigl\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}}\notin\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i_{1}}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i_{n}}}\}\Bigr\}.

Let
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
denote
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}E}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}
or
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}E}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B}
.
For each singleton cluster of
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
,
5
for
\mathsf{U}_{\mathfrak{H}}
follows from
5
for the same cluster of
\mathfrak{G}
for
\mathsf{U}
. All non-singleton clusters of
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
other than
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
have
2n\mathord{-}1
unfolded copies by
6
for the
(2n\mathord{-}1)
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
, and any
n
of them satisfy
6
requisite for an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
. Finally, for cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
of
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
, label sets
{\hat{C}}_{i_{1}},\dots,{\hat{C}}_{i_{n}}
serve as the requisite
n
unfolded copies of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
in
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}
, in particular,
{{}_{\mathfrak{H}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i_{j}}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{G}^{\mathfrak{H}}\mathstrut{}^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
for
j=1..n
. Thus, all
2^{2n\mathord{-}1}
premises spawned by
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
are
n
-unfoldings of either
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
or
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}
.

6.

The case when
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\and B}^{\circ}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G^{\prime\prime}}

is analogous, with
\mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\circ}
used instead of
\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
.

7.

The case when
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A\mathbin{\supset}B}^{\bullet}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
was added to obtain
\mathfrak{G^{\prime\prime}}
is also analogous, with
\mathbin{\supset}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
used instead of
\mathbin{\vee}_{\mathsf{k}}^{\bullet}
.

For each of the steps, we have extended the given tidy derivation
\mathcal{D}_{N}
to a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
in such a way that no labels, forbidden labels, or relational atoms were modified. Since all added rules touch only allowed labels, the resulting derivation is tidy. Thus, derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
is an
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
. Moreover, whenever
\mathcal{D}_{N}
was allowed for some label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
, so is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}
because no label has changed its forbidden/allowed status in the premises.
∎

Lemma .9

(restate = SoundSemiSat, name = )
.

1.

If
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{0}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\ast}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable.

2.

For each
i
, if
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable
and
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\ast}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
for some semi-saturated
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable.

Proof.

1.

If
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{0}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\ast}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{0}=\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{1}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}\cdots\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{k}=\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
for stable sets
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}
, …,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{k}
for some
k\geq 0
. Note all sequents
\mathfrak{G}
appearing in these sets are proper with
\ell(\mathfrak{G})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}\}
because semi-saturation steps neither add labels to the only sequent
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}
with the single label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
nor create clusters.

To show the base of induction, i.e., that
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}=\{\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})\}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable, it is sufficient to consider a tidy derivation
\mathcal{D}^{0}
of the restricted version of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
where label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
is allowed and no rules are applied (note that the only label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
in each of the sequents forms the only layer, which is, therefore, topmost and not inner). As discussed earlier, this one derivation is an
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{0}
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
via the binary relation
\mathsf{U}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r})\}
that is an
n
-unfolding relation for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that
\mathcal{D}^{0}
is clearly allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
.

Step of induction. Let derivations
\mathcal{D}^{i}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
be
n
-unfoldings of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i}
that are allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
. For the semi-saturation step
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i\mathord{+}1}
we can apply Lemma
.8
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
to obtain derivations
\mathcal{D}^{i\mathord{+}1}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
that are
n
-unfoldings of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i\mathord{+}1}
and are allowed for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
.
5
5

5

Since all sequents involved in sets
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i}
are proper, a closer observation of Lemma
.8
shows that one derivation can serve as
n
-unfoldings of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}_{i}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

2.

Recall that according to step 5) of the algorithm, Def.
7.14
, and Constr.
7.10
,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i+1}^{\prime}=\bigl(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}\setminus\{\mathfrak{G}\}\bigr)\cup\{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}\}
for some fully saturated non-axiomatic
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
, where

\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}=\mathfrak{G}+\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{1}}}+\cdots+\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{h}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{h}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{h}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{h}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
are all the unhappy formulas in some of the topmost layers of
\mathfrak{G}
and each
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{j}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
is either a
\mathbin{\supset}
- or a
\mathord{\Box}
-formula.
Also note that since
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
is semi-saturated, we only need to semi-saturate subset
\{\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}\}\subseteq\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i+1}^{\prime}
to obtain
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
.

It might seem natural to split
2
into several finer-grained statements stating that unfolding can be preserved by smaller steps, including many of the steps already covered by Lemma
.8
and adding to them new steps corresponding to Construction
7.9
that create one new layer based on one of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{j}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
. Unfortunately, these intermediate stages cannot be formulated as unfoldings due to the sequent ceasing to be stable because as soon as the first of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{j}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
in a layer is made happy, this layer turns into an inner one, so the sequent does not become stable again until all remaining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{j}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
in this layer are happy.

At the same time, applying all requisite instances of
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
and
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
to obtain an
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i+1}^{\prime}
and then proceeding as in Case
1
, repeatedly applying Lemma
.8
is met with another technical problem. It would violate the tidiness conditions (see Def.
9.7
): each
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
- and
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
-instance creates a new layer and renders the layers created by the previous
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
- or
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
-instances forbidden, so that the rules needed to unfold the semi-saturation steps for those layers cannot be applied anymore. For this reason, we have to apply the necessary rules for unfolding the semi-saturation after each
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
- or
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
-instance.

Thus, we will still follow individual smaller steps in a specific order maintaining conditions
1
–
7
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
after each, but are forced to lump them together into
2
, at the end of which the result can once again be called an unfolding.

Let
L_{1},\ldots,L_{h}
be the layers of
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}
that are introduced by
\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{1}}},\ldots,\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{h}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{h}}}
respectively.
Since
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
is semi-saturated and each rewriting step of
\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}
is local to a layer, we can therefore without loss of generality assume that the rewrite sequence
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\ast}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
is adding formulas layerwise, i.e., all formulas that are added to layer
L_{j}
are added before adding formulas to layer
L_{j^{\prime}}
, whenever
j<j^{\prime}
.

For each step semi-saturating the next layer
L_{j}
we preserve
1
–
7
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the same way as in Case
1
using the fact that
L_{j}
is created of allowed labels that are not made forbidden while unfolding semi-saturation steps. However, formally we cannot rely on Lemma
.8
because of the lack of stability mentioned before. Instead, we simply repeat the arguments from the proof of Lemma
.8
noting that the only part of stability used in its proof was
R\mathsf{tr}
-structural completeness, which does hold upon creating layer
L_{j}
.

The only remaining steps to cover are the creation of layers
L_{j}
. This is done by simply applying the
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
- or
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
-rule to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F_{j}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
. However, in order to preserve the
5
and
6
conditions, we have to start with an
(2n+1)
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
in order to obtain an
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{T}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{T}
. For every lifted cluster that does not contain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}
the additional
n+1
copies in the unfolding are discarded. But when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}
is in a cluster, then
L_{j}
contains 2 copies of that cluster (see Figure
5
), whose
n
repetitions are given to us by the copies
1..n
and
n+1..2n+1
of our lifting. In the
n
th copy, only the lifting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{j}}
is part of the unfolding relation.

It is easy to check that at the end of this process all the conditions of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldability are restored.
∎

The next step is to show that
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}
preserves the property of being unfoldable. For this, we need to repeat parts of the proof.
To simplify the process of repeating, we use the notion of embedding.

Definition .10

(Embedding)
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}
and
\mathfrak{H}
be restricted proper sequents. An
embedding

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
is an injective function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\ell(\mathfrak{G})\to\ell(\mathfrak{H})
obeying the following conditions for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in\ell(\mathfrak{G})
:

1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}
;

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})R_{\mathfrak{H}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y})
;

3.

L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
iff
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y})}}
.

4.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})
has no past in
\mathfrak{H}
;

5.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\mathcal{f}(\mathfrak{G})
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\mathcal{f}(\mathfrak{H})
;

Proposition .11
.

Embeddings are closed under composition and preserve unfoldings.

Proof.

First, we prove that a composition of two embeddings is an embedding. Let
\mathfrak{G}
,
\mathfrak{H}
, and
\mathfrak{J}
be restricted proper sequents. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{J}
be embeddings from
\mathfrak{G}
to
\mathfrak{H}
and from
\mathfrak{H}
to
\mathfrak{J}
respectively. We need to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
is an embedding from
\mathfrak{G}
to
\mathfrak{J}
, where
(e^{\prime}e)({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\bigr)
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\ell({\mathfrak{G}})
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\ell({\mathfrak{G}})\to\ell({\mathfrak{H}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\colon\ell({\mathfrak{H}})\to\ell({\mathfrak{J}})
are injective functions, it is immediate that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\ell({\mathfrak{G}})\to\ell({\mathfrak{J}})
is an injective function. We have for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in\ell({\mathfrak{G}})
:

1

{}_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{}_{\mathfrak{H}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\sim{}_{\mathfrak{J}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\bigr)
.

2

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(x)}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{H}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(y)}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(x)\bigr)}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{J}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(y)\bigr)}
.

3

L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}
iff

L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(x)}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(y)}}
iff

L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(x))}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{J}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(y))}}
.

4

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}

iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})
has no past in
\mathfrak{H}

iff

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\bigr)
has no past in
\mathfrak{J}
.

5

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\mathcal{f}({\mathfrak{G}})

iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\mathcal{f}({\mathfrak{H}})

iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\bigr)\in\mathcal{f}({\mathfrak{J}})
.

Secondly, let
\mathsf{U}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}})\times\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{}}{\hat{G}}})
be an
n
-unfolding relation witnessing that
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
is an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
. We show that binary relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U}\subseteq\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}})\times\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{}}{\hat{H}}})
witnesses that
\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}
is an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad(\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})\Bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})\Bigr).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}})
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}}
.

1

{{}_{\mathfrak{G}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}}
by
1
for
\mathsf{U}
and
{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})}}
by
1
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
.

2

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
be from different clusters. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}}
by
2
for
\mathsf{U}
and the latter iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(\hat{x})}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(\hat{x}^{\prime})}
by
2
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
.

3

L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}
iff
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}}}
by
3
for
\mathsf{U}
, and the latter iff
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(\hat{x})}}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}}}}L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(\hat{x}^{\prime})}}
by
3
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
.

4

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
has no past in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
by
4
for
\mathsf{U}
, and the latter iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}(\hat{x})}
has no past in
\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}
by
4
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
.

5

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
forms a singleton cluster in
\mathfrak{G}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
is the only label in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
by
5
for
\mathsf{U}
. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})
is the only label in
\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})
.

6

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
is a non-singleton cluster in
\mathfrak{G}
with
\left|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\right|=k\geq 2
, then by
6
for
\mathsf{U}
there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{i}}\in\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{G}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i,j}}\in\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}})
for

i=1..k
and
j=1..n

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{k}}\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{i}}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i,j}}
for
i=1..k
,
j=1..n
, and in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,1}}R\cdots R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,1}}R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,2}}R\cdots R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,2}}R\cdots R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,n}}R\cdots R{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,n}}.

In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{i}}\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\mathsf{U})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i,j}})
for
i=1..k
,
j=1..n
and in
\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}
by
2
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,1}})\,R\cdots R\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,1}})\,R\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,2}})\,R\cdots R\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,2}})\,R\cdots\\
R\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{1,n}})\,R\cdots R\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{k,n}}).

Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}_{i,j}})\in\ell(\mathfrak{\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}})
for
i=1..k
,
j=1..n
provide the requisite
n
unfoldings for the labels of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
in
\mathfrak{{\hat{H}}}
.

7

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}})
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}^{\prime}}
by the injectivity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
by
7
for
\mathsf{U}
.∎

Lemma .12

(Embedding Lemma)
.

Assume we have a tidy derivation
\mathcal{D}
with a restricted proper endsequent
\mathfrak{G}
and premises
\mathfrak{G}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{G}_{n}
. If there is an embedding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
into a restricted proper sequent
\mathfrak{H}
, then there is a tidy derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
with conclusion
\mathfrak{H}
and premises
\mathfrak{H}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{H}_{n}
such that there are embeddings
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{i}\colon\mathfrak{G}_{i}\to\mathfrak{H}_{i}
for
i=1..n
.

Proof.

Let us first consider the case where
\mathcal{D}
consists of only one tidy
inference rule instance
\mathsf{r}
. Let
\mathfrak{G}_{i}
be a premise of

\mathsf{r}
. Then, by Proposition
9.4
, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{i}=\mathfrak{G}+\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\prime}
for some
\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\prime}
. If
\mathsf{r}
does not add new labels,
then
\ell({\mathfrak{G}_{i}})=\ell(\mathfrak{G})
, and we have
\ell({\mathfrak{H}_{i}})=\ell(\mathfrak{H})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}
. This gives us a correct rule
application satisfying the lemma. Otherwise, if
\mathsf{r}
adds new
labels, then
\mathsf{r}
is one of
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
,
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
, or
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
, there is only
one premise, and the new labels only occur in
\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\prime}
.

•

\mathsf{r}
is
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
: The rule applies to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}

in
\mathfrak{G}
, creating a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, and we can apply the
same rule to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
in
\mathfrak{H}
, creating a
new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}
. We can define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}
, and
for all other
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in\ell({\mathfrak{G}_{1}})
we define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})
.

•

\mathsf{r}
is
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
or
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
: The rule applies to

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
in
\mathfrak{G}
, creating a new layer
L^{\prime}
whose labels
all occur only in
\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{G}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}}
. We can apply the same
rule to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\circ}}}
in
\mathfrak{H}
, creating a new layer

L^{\prime\prime}
and adding
\mathfrak{H}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{H}\mathord{\uparrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}}
to
\mathfrak{H}
. In
general,
L^{\prime\prime}
can contain more labels than
L^{\prime}
. But
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}

defines an embedding of
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
into
L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})}}
,
which canonically defines a mapping
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\colon L^{\prime}\to L^{\prime\prime}
as follows: for

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}^{\prime}\in L^{\prime}
, we define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime\prime}}\in L^{\prime\prime}
iff either there is
a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\in L_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G_{\rm 1}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v})}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{H_{\rm 1}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y^{\prime\prime}}}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}^{\prime}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}^{\prime\prime}
has
no past in
\mathfrak{H}_{1}
. We can then define our embedding

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{G}_{1}\to\mathfrak{H}_{1}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})
if

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in L^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})
otherwise.

We can now prove the lemma for arbitrary
\mathcal{D}
with a straightforward induction on the height of
\mathcal{D}
.
∎

Lemma .13
.

Whenever for a semi-saturated sequent
\mathfrak{G}
, the algorithm in step 1) applies Option
1
) of Def.
7.5
by substituting label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
for label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
in
\mathfrak{G}
and applying the transitive closure of
R
to obtain
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
, label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
forms a singleton cluster in
\mathfrak{G}
.

Proof.

Since the application of Option
1
) means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
has no past in
\mathfrak{G}
, it follows that the algorithm created
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
as a singleton cluster (unless
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}
, in which case it was present from the very beginning, but was a singleton cluster at first). It is not hard to check that every time the algorithm creates a new nonsingleton cluster,either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
have a past for all label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
in the cluster or all labelled formulas
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}B^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
become happy for all label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
in the cluster. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
has to be unhappy and have no past to apply Option
1
), label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
is not part of a nontrivial cluster.
∎

Definition .14
.

For a label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
of a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
we define

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{x}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\}.

Lemma .15

(restate = SoundSat, name = )
.

If
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\Diamond}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}
and
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is semi-saturated and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable
, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable.

Proof.

Recall that according to Def.
7.5
,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}
here is a semi-saturation of a set
\bigl(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\setminus\{\mathfrak{G}\}\bigr)\cup\{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\}
for some
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
such that
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\Diamond}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. Note that
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is a stable sequent by Lemma
.5
(
1
). The semi-saturation is handled as in Lemma
.9
using the fact that
\mathfrak{G}
was semi-saturated and, hence, the only label that semi-saturation is going to touch is a new label, which is created as allowed. Thus, what remains is to explain how to turn
N
-unfoldings of
\mathfrak{G}
into
n
-unfoldings of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
for appropriate
N\geq n
.
According to Definition
7.5
there are two options:

1.

In Option 1, we have
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{\bullet}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
forms a singleton cluster by Lemma
.13
, and we have some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
from a topmost layer with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
such that all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{x}}
have no past. Then
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}

is obtained by substituting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and closing under

R
-transitivity. Note that the resulting
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is semi-saturated, hence,
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}=\bigl(\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\setminus\{\mathfrak{G}\}\bigr)\cup\{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\}
.
Consider any premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
that is an
N
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
via
\mathsf{U}_{1}
. By
5
, there is a unique
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. The choice of
N
and the extension of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
depend on the size of the cluster that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to in
\mathfrak{G}
:

(a)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{x\}}
is a singleton cluster, set
N\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}n
. By
5
, there is a unique
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. By
2
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
has no past by assumption, by
4
neither has
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. It follows from tree-layeredness of
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
that all labels in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{\hat{x}}}
, including
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
, have no past. Hence, all labels in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{\hat{x}}}
, with the possible exception of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
itself, are created by rule
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
. Let
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
be the premise of the first (i.e., lowermost) instance of rule
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
on the branch of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
leading to
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
that creates a non-trivial child of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
, let us call this child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}_{1}
. Since
\mathcal{D}_{n}
is tidy, label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}_{1}
is the only allowed label in
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
be the principal formula of this instance of
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
. Then
\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!u_{1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}C}^{\bullet}
is the only labelled formula in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{1}}
. Since
\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
, also
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{x}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
higher up the branch, hence,
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
by
1
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
and
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
by
1
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
is in a topmost layer of
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
, a tidy rule
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
can be applied to
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
as its principal formula. Let the premise of this rule be
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{2}}
be the new label created by this rule, the only allowed label in
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
. Consider the following function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}\colon\ell(\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{1}}})\to\ell(\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{2}}})
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}),({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{2}})\}\cup\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\in\ell(\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{1}}\}\}.

It is easy to see that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}
is an embedding. The tidy derivation subtree rooted at
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
can be applied to
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
via this embedding resulting in a tidy derivation by Lemma
.12
with all the premises of the extension being
n
-unfoldings of the same sequents from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
as the respective premises of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
by Prop.
.11
. In particular, for premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
of the subtree, embedding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}
will be extended to en embedding into some premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{2}
of the extended derivation with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{1}(\hat{y})=\hat{y}_{2}
such that
{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{2}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}}
(note that
{{}_{\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{2}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\sim{{}_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}\mathstrut^{\mathstrut}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}}
). By Prop.
.11
, we can construct an
n
-unfolding
\mathsf{U}_{2}
of
\mathfrak{G}
into
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{2}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathsf{U}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathsf{U}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}
for all labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}\in\ell(\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{1}}\}=\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\}
, including for all non-trivial parents of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. Further,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
and, whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathsf{U}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{\mathrm{2}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
. In particular,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{\mathrm{2}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{\mathrm{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{y}_{2}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}C}^{\bullet}
.

We can apply
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
to obtain
\mathfrak{K}_{3}
with a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{3}}
, take an embedding

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{2}}),({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{3}})\}\cup\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w})\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\in\ell(\mathfrak{K_{\mathrm{2}}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{2}}\}\}.

from
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
into
\mathfrak{K}_{3}
and repeat the process.

After
n-1
such repetitions, we will get a premise
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{n\mathord{+}1}
and an
n
-unfolding
\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}
of
\mathfrak{G}
into
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{n\mathord{+}1}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathsf{U}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}
for all labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}\in\ell(\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}\}
, including for all non-trivial parents of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
. Further,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n}}
and, whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
. In particular,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y_{n\mathord{+}1}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n}}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}
.

Once again, all other premises of this derivation expanded
n
times are
n
-unfoldings of sequents from
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
. It remains to turn
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{n\mathord{+}1}
into an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. We do this by modifying
\mathsf{U}_{n+1}
for the newly created cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}R_{\mathfrak{G}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\}
in
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and keeping unfoldings of all other labels of
\mathfrak{G}
as in
\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}
. For each label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
and each
j=1..n
there is at least one label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathsf{U}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j\mathord{-}1}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}
. These labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{1}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{n}}
for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
can, thus, be taken as the requisite
n
unfolding copies of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
. Indeed, whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\mathsf{U}_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}w}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, as discussed, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\hat{w}}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}
. On the other hand, whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\mathsf{U}_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\}
, as discussed, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n}}R_{\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{\mathrm{n\mathord{+}1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{v}}
. Note that children of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
that are not in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
in
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
must be unfolded into labels that are children of all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{u}_{j}}
, which is why we needed to create
n+1
successful unfoldings rather than
n
: the first
n
unfoldings are used to create
n
unfolded copies of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
whereas the last
(n\mathord{+}1)
th provides unfoldings for children of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
that are not in its cluster.

(b)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
belongs to a non-singleton cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
, we use a similar expansion to the case of singleton clusters just discussed. Hence, we will only outline the differences. Firstly, instead of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
we start with
\mathcal{D}_{N}
for
N=\max(n,2)
. The fact that
N\geq n
guarantees
n
-unfoldings for all premises other than
\mathfrak{G}
and for all clusters of
\mathfrak{G}
other than the newly created
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
that contains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
and all labels in between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
excluding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
.

Among the
N\geq 2
unfolding copies of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
existing by
6
, use the penultimate,
(N\mathord{-}1)
th unfolding copy as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
and the unique unfolding of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. For these
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
perform the expansion as in the case for a singleton cluster. The only difference to that case is how the final unfolding
\mathsf{U}_{n\mathord{+}1}
is to be modified to account for the newly created cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C^{\prime}_{x}}
. For all labels outside of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
, the construction remains the same. For each label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{x}}
, its last,
N
th unfolding copy from
\mathsf{U}_{1}
is among labels repeated in the segment (note that the same need not be true about the
(N\mathord{-}1)
th unfolding copy, which is why we had to start one unfolding copy earlier). Hence, this
N
th copy is now repeated
n\mathord{+}1
times, and the first
n
of these are taken as new unfoldings of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
.

2.

In Option 2, we have
\mathfrak{G},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!y}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{\bullet}
and create a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
such that
\mathfrak{G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!z}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}A}^{\bullet}
. This case is similar to the steps taken for the semi-saturation in Lemma
.8
, which is also used to perform the semi-saturation after
\mathfrak{G}
is replaced by
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
, as discussed earlier.
We provide an abbreviated description how to expand
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
that is an
N
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}
into an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
. Once again, the exact procedure depends on the size of the cluster that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
belongs to in
\mathfrak{G}
:

(a)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{y\}}
is a singleton cluster, use
N=n
. By
5
, there is a unique
\hat{y}
such that
y\mathsf{U}\hat{y}
. By
1
,
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{\bullet}
for this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}
. It is sufficient to apply a tidy instance of
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
to this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
creating a fresh label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
, the only allowed label in the premise
and set
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{U}\cup\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}})\}
. It is easy to check that all unfolding conditions are satisfied for the resulting premise to be an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
for some cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
with
\left|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\right|=k\geq 2
, use
N=n\mathord{+}1
. By
6
, sequent
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
contains
n\mathord{+}1
unfolded copies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}}
for
j=1..n\mathord{+}1
in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}=\mathsf{U}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{j}})
for
j=1..n\mathord{+}1
. By
1
,
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A}^{\bullet}
. Here we apply a tidy instance of
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}_{\star}
to this
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{y}_{n\mathord{+}1}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}\mathord{\Diamond}A^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bullet}}}
creating a fresh label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
, the only allowed label in the premise
and modify
\mathsf{U}
as follows:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
is made the unique unfolding of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}
;

•

unfoldings of all labels from
\ell(\mathfrak{G})\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
remain intact;

•

for the unfoldings of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
we only keep the first
n
unfoldings guaranteed by
6
for
\mathsf{U}
removing all others.

The removal of the extra unfoldings in the last item is necessary to make sure that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{z}}
is a child of all unfoldings of cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}
. Once again, it is easy to check that all unfolding conditions are satisfied for the resulting premise to be an
n
-unfolding of
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
.∎

Lemma .16

(restate = SoundLoop, name = )
.

If
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\circlearrowright}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}
and
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is saturated and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable, then
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{{\hat{S}}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathfrak{G}\mathbin{\rightsquigarrow_{\circlearrowright}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{G}\in\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}

saturated and
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable, and
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}=\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}\setminus\{\mathfrak{G}\}\cup\{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\}
. We want to
construct an
n
-unfolding for
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
.
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
is obtained
from
\mathfrak{G}
by closing an unhappy triangle loop. Let

L_{1},L_{2},L^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}

as in Definitions
7.16
and
7.17
.
We also have
a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}}
, that we will use in the proof.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
are in the same cluster, then every

n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
is also an
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
. Now, assume that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
are not in the same
cluster.

As in case 1b) of the previous lemma, we start from an
N
-unfolding
\mathcal{D}_{N}
of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}
, where
N=\max(n,2)
and
let
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
be a premise of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
that is an unfolding of

\mathfrak{G}
. Let
\mathsf{U}
be the corresponding unfolding relation and let
{\hat{L}}_{1},{\hat{L}}_{2},{\hat{L}}^{\prime}
be the unfoldings of

L_{1},L_{2},L^{\prime}
, respectively.

We now let
\mathcal{T}
be the subtree of
\mathcal{D}_{N}
rooted at the sequent where
{\hat{L}}^{\prime}
first occurs. Let
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{0}
be that sequent, let
\mathsf{r}
be the instance of the inference rule (an
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
or
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
) that introduced
{\hat{L}}^{\prime}
, let
\mathfrak{H}
be the conclusion of that rule instance, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{1}}
be the label in
\mathfrak{H}
containing the formula to which that rule was applied. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{1}}
. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
be a label in
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}\mathsf{U}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}
is a singleton, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
is uniquely defined. Otherwise, we pick for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
the
k
th repetition of the cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{2}}
, where
k
is the repetion of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
in which
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{1}}
occurred. We can apply the same rule
\mathsf{r}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
to obtain the layer
{\hat{L}}^{\prime\prime}
. Let the new sequent be
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{1}
.

We also let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}^{\prime}_{1}}
be the future of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{1}}
in
{\hat{L}}^{\prime}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}^{\prime}_{2}}
be the future of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2}}
in
{\hat{L}}^{\prime\prime}
. This allows us to define an embedding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{e}}\colon\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{0}\to\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}_{1}
acting like the identity on all layers, except
{\hat{L}}^{\prime}
which is embedded into
{\hat{L}}^{\prime\prime}
mapping each label to its unique future, except for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{\hat{p}_{1}}}
which is embedded into
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\overrightarrow{\hat{p}_{2}}}
. We are now going to apply the Embedding Lemma
.12
to repeat the proof tree
\mathcal{T}
. More precisely, let
\mathfrak{H}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{H}_{m}
be the leaves of
\mathcal{T}
and let
\mathfrak{H}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{H}_{l}
for some
l\leq m
be the leaves of
\mathcal{T}
that are
N
-unfoldings of
\mathfrak{G}
(and
\mathfrak{{\hat{G}}}
is among them).
Each of
\mathfrak{H}_{i}
has a topmost layer
{\hat{L}}_{2,i}
that is an
N
-unfolding of
L_{2}
, and that has the labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{s}_{i}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{t}_{i}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{p}_{2,i}}
, corresponding to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{2}}
in the unfolding (if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
is in a cluster, we pick for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{s}_{i}}
the first repetition; if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
is in a cluster, we pick for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{t}_{i}}
the last repetition).

We proceed now similarly to the proof of Lemma
.15
by plugging in a copy of the subtree

\mathcal{T}
at each leaf
\mathfrak{H}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{H}_{l}
. Note that whenever a new layer is created and the previous one is lifted using the
\mathbin{\supset}^{\circ}_{\star}
and
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}_{\star}
-rules, also the gap between the old an the new triangle is lifted (see Fig.
13
). We call
{\hat{L}}_{3,i}
the new topmost layer (which is a copy of
{\hat{L}}_{2,i}
with and additional part
{\hat{L}}^{\ast}_{3,i}
that is a copy of labels in
{\hat{L}}_{2,i}
but that have no counterpart in
{\hat{L}}_{1}
.
If a rule instance in
\mathcal{T}
is adding a formula to one of the labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{h_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}\mathbin{R_{\mathfrak{h_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
then we need do the same to the corresponding label in
{\hat{L}}^{\ast}_{3,i}
. Formally, this can be achieved via a second embdding for each
i
, applying again Lemma
.12
.

As in the previous Lemma
.15
, we now repeat this construction
n
times, so that we obtain our desired
n
-unfolding of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
.
∎

\UnfoldingLemma

*

Proof.

The set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{0}=\{\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})\}
is trivially
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable. By the previous lemmas, this property is preserved by all the steps that are used to construct the sequence
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{0},\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{1},\ldots
.
∎

\Provable

*

Proof.

By the previous lemma, it follows that the axiomatic set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{k}
that caused the algorithm to terminate is
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
-unfoldable. In particular, we have a derivation in
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
with conclusion
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
where all premises are 1-unfoldings of elements of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{k}
, which are all axiomatic. Therefore we can use instances of the
\mathsf{id}
and
\mathord{\bot}^{\bullet}
rules to give a complete proof of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
in
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\star}
. By Proposition
9.4
, there is also a proof of
\mathfrak{G}_{0}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F})
in
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\prime}
. This we can append with rules
R\mathsf{rf}
and
\mathord{\leq}\mathsf{rf}
at the bottom to get the proof of
\Longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}r}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
in
\mathsf{lab}\mathsf{IS4}_{\leq}^{\prime}
. By Corollary
4.3
and Theorem
4.1
, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
is a theorem of
\mathsf{IS4}
.
∎

-G

Proofs from Section
10

\sizelabel

*

Proof.

Only subformulas of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
can occur in a sequent. Furthermore, the
position of each subformula occurrence in
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
determines if such a subformula
can occur on the left or on the right of the
\Longrightarrow
.
Moreover, the algorithm introduces in a sequent only labelled formulas which do not already occur in the sequent (Def.
7.1
).
Hence, we can have at most
n

formula occurrences at a given label.
Also note that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
contain the same set of
subformulas of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
. Hence, there are
2^{n}
different equivalence
classes for
\sim
on labels
which can occur in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
.
∎

\sizecluster

*

Proof.

We shall show that,
by construction, all labels in a cluster are different with respect to
\sim
.
There are two ways of introducing non-singleton clusters: (i)
via unhappy
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}
-formulas (Option
1
of Definition
7.5
):
if there are equivalent labels in such a cluster, the cluster would have been created
at an earlier step of the algorithm.
(ii) via unhappy R-triangle or U-triangle loops (Definitions
7.16
,
7.17
and
7.18
): if at creation the chain from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
contains duplicates, then they are collapsed immediately afterwards, as they are also valid choices of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
, fulfilling the
same unhappy triangle conditions. Hence, when
\mathbin{\mathbin{\lower 1.07639pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\rightsquigarrow$\hss}\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{$\rightsquigarrow$}}_{\circlearrowright}}
terminates, in all new clusters all labels are different with respect to
\sim
. This limits the size of clusters to
2^{n}
, and we have at most
2^{2^{n}}-1
different equivalence classes
of non-empty clusters which can occur in
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
.
∎

\lengthbranch

*

Fig. 14:
A branch and its pasts

Proof.

A branch
M^{\prime}
is a
past
of a branch
M
in a sequent
\mathfrak{G}
, if for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
there is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in M
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
. In this case we write
M^{\prime}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}M
.
For a branch
M
we write
\check{M}
for the labels in
M
that have a
past, and
\bar{M}
for the ones that do not have a past. Then

M=\check{M}\cup\bar{M}
and
\check{M}\cap\bar{M}=\varnothing
. We now
consider a branch
M
in a topmost layer
L
and all its
pasts, i.e., branches
M^{\prime}
with
M^{\prime}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}M
.

Let
M_{0},M_{1},\ldots,M_{k}
be all the pasts of
M
for which
\bar{M}_{i}\neq\varnothing
, and such that
M_{i}\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}M_{j}
whenever
0\leq i\leq j\leq k
(see Fig.
14
).
We have
\left|\bar{M}_{i}\right|\leq 2^{n}+1
for all
i
because every label in
\bar{M}_{i}

is created by Option
2
of Definition
7.5
, in correspondence to an unhappy
\mathord{\Diamond}^{\bullet}
-formula (except for the first label in
\bar{M}_{i}
, which could be created by layer lifting in correspondence to an unhappy
\mathord{\Box}^{\circ}
formula (Construction
7.9
), or could be the initial label created in Step 0 of the algorithm). But in any case, after at most
2^{n}
such steps, we encounter a label which is equivalent to a previous one (see also the proof of Lemma
7.6
.
2
).

Now let
{M}^{i}_{j}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in M_{i}\mid\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\bar{M}_{j}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{\mathbin{\leq_{\mathfrak{G}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}\}
be the set of futures of
\bar{M}_{j}
in
M_{i}

for some
j<i
(see Fig.
14
). Because of the duplication of clusters in the
layer lifting (Construction
7.8
), we can have

\left|{M}^{i}_{j}\right|>\left|\bar{M}_{j}\right|
. We can restrict the size of

{M}^{i}_{j}
because there is only a limited amount of times a
cluster can be duplicated before
a U-triangle loops is encountered.
Then,
the length of the branch from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
before and
after the repetition of the cluster is again bound by
2\cdot(2^{n}+1)
, because
otherwise the U-triangle loop would be unhappy. (We have to take
2^{n}+1
twice because the suricata label is not allowed to occur between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}
.)
Since the size of a
cluster is bound by
2^{n}
, and the number of clusters in
\bar{M}_{j}

is bound by
2^{n}
, the size of
{M}^{i}_{j}
is bound by
2\cdot 2\cdot 2^{n}\cdot 2^{n}\cdot 2^{n}=2^{3n+2}
.
(This is a very naive
argument, and the number could be restricted to a much smaller
one. However, this paper is already technical enough, and we are
here only interested in the fact that such a bound exists.)

Let us now put a bound to
k
. Very naively, we see an R-triangle loop after
k_{\mathrm{max}}=2^{2^{n}}
(as we need to repeat the cluster
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C_{1}}
, see Definition
7.16
and Lemma
10.2
). Let
M_{j}
be the branch
that sees a repetition of
M_{i}
. Define
\bar{M}_{i/j}
(for
j<i
) to be the set of labels in
M_{i}
that do not have
a past in
M_{j}
(see Fig.
14
). Then the R-triangle loop is unhappy if
\left|\bar{M}_{i/j}\right|\geq 2\cdot 2^{n}=2^{n+1}
(same argument as above). Since
2^{3n+2}>2^{n+1}
, we have that
\left|M\right|\leq k_{\mathrm{max}}\cdot\left|{M}^{i}_{j}\right|=2^{n}\cdot 2^{2^{n}}\cdot 2^{3n+2}=2^{2^{n}+4n+2}
.

Note that as soon we observe an unhappy triangle loop, the size of the newly created cluster is
\left|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}\right|\leq 2^{n}
.
∎

\TerminationAlg

*

Proof.

By the previous lemma, the size of a branch in a layer is bounded by
the formula
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.6,0.1}F}
in the end sequent. Hence the number of possible
layers that can occur in a sequent in a set
\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\hbox to0.0pt{$\mathfrak{S}$\hss}\mkern 0.75mu\mathfrak{S}_{i}
that is
visited by the algorithm is also bounded. This puts a limit to the
height of the tree of layers in the sequents, as eventually there
will be a simulation by a previous layer.∎
